.

4.2.2
Quotient Geometries for
{\cal M}_{r}

In this section, we introduce three quotient structures for
{\cal M}_{r}
based on three fixed-rank matrix factorizations. For
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\cal M}_{r}
, denote the SVD as
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\prime}{\mathbf{V}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}
.

(1) Full-rank Factorization
\mathcal{M}_{r}^{q_{1}}
.
In this factorization, we rearrange the SVD of
{\mathbf{X}}
as
{\mathbf{X}}=({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{{}^{\prime}1/2})(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{{}^{\prime}1/2}{\mathbf{V}}^{{}^{\prime}\top})={\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}
, where
{\mathbf{L}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r}_{*}
,
{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}_{*}
. The first quotient geometry for
{\cal M}_{r}
results from the invariance mapping
({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})\mapsto({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})
for
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\rm GL}(r)
, where
{\rm GL}(r):=\{{\mathbf{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}:{\mathbf{M}}\text{ is invertible}\}
denotes the degree
r
general linear group. It is thus straightforward to consider the equivalence classes
[{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}]=\{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}):{\mathbf{M}}\in{\rm GL}(r)\}
as the search space. The set of equivalence classes form the quotient manifold
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{1}}:=\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}/{\rm GL}(r)
, where
\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}=\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{1}\times r}\times\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}
, as
{\rm GL}(r)
is a Lie group
[
Lee13
, Theorem 21.10]
. Suppose
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}},{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}
are two
r
-by-
r
positive definite matrices depending on
{\mathbf{L}}
and
{\mathbf{R}}
, the metric we endow on
T_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
is
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}^{r}(\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}\eta_{L}^{\top}\theta_{R})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}\eta_{R}^{\top}\theta_{R})
for
\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}=[\eta_{L}^{\top}\quad\eta_{R}^{\top}]^{\top},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}=[\theta_{L}^{\top}\quad\theta_{R}^{\top}]^{\top}\in T_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
. In the following Lemma
7
, we show with some proper assumptions on
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}
,
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{1}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold.

Lemma 7
.

(i) Suppose
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1})
and
{\mathbf{V}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{2})
span the top
r
left and right singular subspaces of
{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}
, respectively and
{\mathbf{P}}_{1}={\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{P}}_{2}={\mathbf{V}}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}
. Then the vertical and horizontal spaces of
T_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
are given as follows:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}&=\left\{\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}=[\theta_{L}^{\top}\quad\theta_{R}^{\top}]^{\top}:\theta_{L}={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}_{2}^{-\top},\theta_{R}=-{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}{\mathbf{P}}_{1}^{-\top},{\mathbf{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}\right\},\\
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}&=\left\{\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}=\begin{bmatrix}\theta_{L}\\
\theta_{R}\end{bmatrix}:\begin{array}[]{l}\theta_{L}=({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}_{2}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}_{2}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}){\mathbf{P}}_{2}^{-\top},{\mathbf{D}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{1}-r)\times r}\\
\theta_{R}=({\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}{\mathbf{P}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}_{1}^{\top}+{\mathbf{V}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}){\mathbf{P}}_{1}^{-\top},{\mathbf{D}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{2}-r)\times r}\end{array},{\mathbf{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}\right\},\end{split}

with
\dim(\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}})=r^{2}
,
\dim(\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}})=(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r
and
\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}\perp\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
with respect to
\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}
.

(ii) Moveover,
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{1}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold endowed with metric
g_{[{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}]}^{r}
induced from
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}^{r}
if and only if
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}={\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}}{\mathbf{M}}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}={\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-1}
hold for any
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\rm GL}(r)
.

Remark 3
.

Similarly to
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}
for the PSD case, we introduce
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}
in
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}^{r}
to accommodate various metric choices considered in literature. Common choices include:
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}=({\mathbf{L}}^{\top}{\mathbf{L}})^{-1},{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}=({\mathbf{R}}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}})^{-1}

[
MBS11a
,
MMBS14
]
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}={\mathbf{R}}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}={\mathbf{L}}^{\top}{\mathbf{L}}

[
MAS12
]
. Distinct from the quotient geometry on
{\cal M}^{q_{1}}_{r+}
, the flat metric, i.e., the Euclidean one with
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}={\mathbf{I}}_{r}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}={\mathbf{I}}_{r}
, is no longer proper as it does not yield a valid Riemannian metric in the quotient space.

(2) Polar Factorization
\mathcal{M}_{r}^{q_{2}}
.
We consider another factorization:
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{O}}({\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\prime}{\mathbf{O}})({\mathbf{V}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{O}})^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}
, where
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r},{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1})
,
{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r),{\mathbf{V}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{2})
. The rotational invariance mapping here is
({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})\mapsto({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})
for
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. This gives us the equivalence classes
[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}}]=\{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}}):{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}\}
and the second quotient manifold
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{2}}=\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}/\mathbb{O}_{r}
, where
\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}={\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)\times{\rm St}(r,p_{2})
. We pick metrics for
{\rm St}(r,p)
and
\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
given in Table
1
with “
\blacklozenge
” as
{\mathbf{B}}
, and endow
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
with the product metric
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}^{r}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{B}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{V}^{\top}\theta_{V})
for
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=[\eta_{U}^{\top}\quad\eta_{B}^{\top}\quad\eta_{V}^{\top}]^{\top},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}\quad\theta_{V}^{\top}]^{\top}\in T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
. For technical convenience, we focus on the case
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{I}}_{r},{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{B}}^{-1}
, which is the most standard metric under this geometry
[
MMBS13
,
MMBS14
]
. In the following Lemma
8
, we show
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{2}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold and provide expressions for its vertical and horizontal spaces.

Lemma 8
.

{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{2}}
endowed with the metric
g^{r}_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}}]}
induced from
\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold with the following vertical and horizontal spaces:

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}&=\left\{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}\quad\theta_{V}^{\top}]^{\top}:\begin{array}[]{c}\theta_{U}={\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega},\theta_{B}={\mathbf{B}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}{\mathbf{B}},\\
\theta_{V}={\mathbf{V}}\boldsymbol{\Omega},\boldsymbol{\Omega}=-\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}\end{array}\right\},\\
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}&=\left\{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}\quad\theta_{V}^{\top}]^{\top}:\begin{array}[]{c}\theta_{U}={\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}+{\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega},\theta_{B}\in\mathbb{S}^{r\times r},\theta_{V}={\mathbf{V}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}-{\mathbf{V}}\boldsymbol{\Omega},\\
\boldsymbol{\Omega}=-\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},{\mathbf{D}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{1}-r)\times r},{\mathbf{D}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{2}-r)\times r}\end{array}\right\},\end{split}

(16)

with
\dim(\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}})=(r^{2}-r)/2
,
\dim(\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}})=(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r
.

(3) Subspace-projection Factorization
\mathcal{M}_{r}^{q_{3}}
.
The third quotient geometry is based on the factorization
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\prime}{\mathbf{V}}^{{}^{\prime}\top})={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}
, where
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1})
and
{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}_{*}
. This factorization is called the subspace-projection factorization
[
MMBS14
]
as
{\mathbf{U}}
represents the column space of
{\mathbf{X}}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}
is the left projection coefficient matrix of
{\mathbf{X}}
on
{\mathbf{U}}
. Here the rotational invariance mapping is
({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})\mapsto({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})
for
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
and the equivalence classes are
[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}}]=\{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}):{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}\}
. This results in the third quotient manifold we are interested in:
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{3}}:=\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}/\mathbb{O}_{r}
, where
\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}={\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}
. By taking the metric on
{\rm St}(r,p_{1})
with “
\blacklozenge
” in
{\mathbf{V}}_{\blacklozenge}
as
{\mathbf{Y}}
, we endow
\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
with the metric
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}^{r}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\eta_{Y}^{\top}\theta_{Y})
for
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[\eta_{U}^{\top}\quad\eta_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top}\in T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
. In the following Lemma
9
, we provide the vertical and horizontal spaces of
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
and show with some proper assumptions on
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}}
,
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{3}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold.

Lemma 9
.

(i)
The vertical and horizontal spaces of
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
are

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}&=\{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top}:\theta_{U}={\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega},\theta_{Y}={\mathbf{Y}}\boldsymbol{\Omega},\boldsymbol{\Omega}=-\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}\},\\
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}&=\{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top}:\theta_{U}={\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}},\theta_{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r},{\mathbf{D}}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{1}-r)\times r}\}.\end{split}

(17)

Here,
\dim(\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}})=(r^{2}-r)/2
,
\dim(\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}})=(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r
.

(ii) Moreover,
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{3}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold endowed with metric
g_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}}]}^{r}
induced from
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}^{r}
if and only if
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
hold for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
.

Remark 4
.

In Lemmas
8
and
9
, we introduce new horizontal spaces that are distinct from the canonical horizontal spaces in the literature in
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{2}}
and
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{3}}

[
AAM14
,
MBS11a
,
MMBS13
,
MMBS14
]
. These new horizontal spaces admit closed-form expressions, which makes developing a correspondence between the embedded manifold tangent space
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r}
and the non-canonical horizontal spaces
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
,
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
easier, and facilitates the later landscape analysis (see later in Propositions
6
and
7
).

The following Lemma
10
shows the above full-rank, polar, and subspace-projection factorizations fully take into account the invariance in the corresponding quotient geometries and its proof is provided in Appendix
C
.

Lemma 10
.

•

(Full-rank Factorization) Let
{\mathbf{L}}_{1},{\mathbf{L}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{1}\times r}
and
{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}
. Then
{\mathbf{L}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{L}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top}
if and only if
{\mathbf{L}}_{2}={\mathbf{L}}_{1}{\mathbf{M}}
and
{\mathbf{R}}_{2}={\mathbf{R}}_{1}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}
for some
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\rm GL}(r)
.

•

(Polar Factorization) Let
{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1})
,
{\mathbf{B}}_{1},{\mathbf{B}}_{2}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{2})
. Then
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}
if and only if
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
,
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}={\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{2}={\mathbf{V}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
for some
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
.

•

(Subspace-projection Factorization) Let
{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1})
and
{\mathbf{Y}}_{1},{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}
. Then
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}^{\top}
if and only if
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}={\mathbf{Y}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
for some
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
.

Finally,
{\cal M}_{r}=\{{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}:{\mathbf{L}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r}_{*},{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}\}=\{{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}:{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1}),{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r),{\mathbf{V}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{2})\}=\{{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}:{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1}),{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}\}
.

In Table
3
, we summarize the basic properties of the full-rank factorization, polar factorization, and subspace-projection factorization based quotient manifolds of the general fixed-rank matrices.

5
Geometric Connections of Embedded and Quotient Geometries in Fixed-rank PSD Matrix Optimization

In this section, we apply the general procedure in Section
3
to connect the landscape properties of optimization (
1
) under the embedded and the quotient geometries. First, under the two quotient geometries introduced in Section
4.2.1
, the optimization problem (
1
) can be reformulated as follows,

\displaystyle\text{on }\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}:

\displaystyle\quad\min_{{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}}):=f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}),

(18a)

\displaystyle\text{on }\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}:

\displaystyle\quad\min_{{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p),{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}):=f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}).

(18b)

Since
\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})
and
\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})
are invariant along the fibers of
\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
and
\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
, they induce functions
h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])
and
h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])
on quotient manifolds
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
and
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
, respectively. Next, we provide the expressions for Riemannian gradients and Hessians of (
1
) under both geometries (Step 1), construct the bijective maps between
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
,
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
(Step 2), and give their spectrum bounds (Step 3).

Proposition 1

(Riemannian Gradients and Hessians of (
1
))
.

The Riemannian gradients and Hessians of (
1
) under the embedded and the quotient geometries introduced in Section
4
are:

•

On
{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
: Suppose
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
,
{\mathbf{U}}
spans the top
r
eigenspace of
{\mathbf{X}}
,
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{S}}&{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{D}}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}\in T_{{\mathbf{X}}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
. Then

\begin{split}{\rm grad}f({\mathbf{X}})&=P_{{\mathbf{U}}}\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})P_{{\mathbf{U}}}+P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})P_{{\mathbf{U}}}+P_{{\mathbf{U}}}\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}},\\
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}},\xi_{\mathbf{X}}]&=\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}},\xi_{\mathbf{X}}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\rangle,\end{split}

(19)

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}={\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{U}}
.

•

On
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
: Suppose
{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
and
\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}}\in\mathcal{H}_{{\mathbf{Y}}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
. Then

\begin{split}\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}&=2\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}){\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1},\\
\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]&=\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top},{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}),\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\rangle\\
&+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}){\mathbf{Y}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}],\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\rangle+\langle{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\left[\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}\right],\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\rangle/2.\end{split}

(20)

•

On
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
: Suppose
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p)
,
{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
and
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
. Then

\begin{split}&\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}=\begin{bmatrix}\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{U}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}\\
\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}2P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\\
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\end{bmatrix},\\
&\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]\\
&=\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}),\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\rangle\\
&\,+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}},2\theta_{U}\theta_{B}+{\mathbf{U}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{B}}-\theta_{U}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}-{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\rangle\\
&\,+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}]\theta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})/2+{\rm tr}({\rm Sym}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}])\theta_{B}).\end{split}

(21)

Remark 5
.

(
Quadratic Form of Riemannian Hessians
) In Proposition
1
, we only give the quadratic expressions of the Hessians as we use them exclusively throughout the paper. It is easy to obtain general bilinear expressions by noting that
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}},\theta_{\mathbf{X}}]=({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}}+\theta_{\mathbf{X}},\xi_{\mathbf{X}}+\theta_{\mathbf{X}}]-{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}}-\theta_{\mathbf{X}},\xi_{\mathbf{X}}-\theta_{\mathbf{X}}])/4
.

We also note the Riemannian Hessian expressions under the fixed-rank quotient geometries have been explicitly or implicitly developed in
[
JBAS10
,
Mey11
,
MMBS14
,
MMS11
]
for some specific problems. Most of these works only provide the linear form of the Riemannian Hessians, which often do not admit a closed-form expression due to the horizontal projection involved in (
5
). Here we provide explicit formulas for the quadratic form of the Riemannian Hessians and the closed-form expressions are critical in establishing the landscape connections of embedded and quotient geometries in fixed-rank matrix optimization.

Proposition 2

(Bijection Between
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
and
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
,
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}
, the eigenspace of
{\mathbf{X}}
is
{\mathbf{U}}
, and
{\mathbf{P}}={\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Y}}
. For any
\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
and
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{S}}&{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{D}}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}\in T_{{\mathbf{X}}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
, define

\begin{split}\xi^{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}_{{\mathbf{X}}}&:=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{P}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}+{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}&{\mathbf{P}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\\
{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}\in T_{{\mathbf{X}}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+},\\
\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}&:=({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}){\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}},\end{split}

(22)

where
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}
in
\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}
is uniquely determined by the linear equation system . Then we can find a linear bijective mapping
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}
between
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
and
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
,

\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}:\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}\longrightarrow\xi_{{\mathbf{X}}}^{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}\quad\text{and}\quad(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+})^{-1}:\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}\to\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}},

such that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})={\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}
holds for any
\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
.

Finally,
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{r+}
satisfies the following spectrum bound:

2\sigma_{r}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})\leq\|\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 4\sigma_{1}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}),\quad\forall\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}.

(23)

Proof of Proposition
2
.
The proof is divided into two steps: in Step 1, we show
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}^{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}
and
\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}
are well defined for any
\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}
and
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}
; in Step 2, we show
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{r+}
is a bijection and prove its spectrum bounds.

Step 1.
First, it is clear for any
\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
,
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}^{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}
is well defined. To show
\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}
is well defined for any
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\in T_{{\mathbf{X}}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
, we need to show the linear system
\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}+\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}^{\top}={\mathbf{S}}
,
\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}^{\top}
has a unique solution with respect to
\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}
. Simple calculations assert that

\begin{split}&\{\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}:\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}+\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}^{\top}={\mathbf{S}},\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}^{\top}\}\\
\subseteq&\{\widetilde{{\mathbf{S}}}:{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\widetilde{{\mathbf{S}}}+\widetilde{{\mathbf{S}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}\}.\end{split}

(24)

Observing facts that

{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\widetilde{{\mathbf{S}}}+\widetilde{{\mathbf{S}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}

(25)

is a Sylvester equation with respect to
\widetilde{{\mathbf{S}}}
, and
{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}
and
-{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}
have disjoint spectrums, we know from
[
Bha13
, Theorem VII.2.1]
that (
25
) has a unique solution, denoted
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}
. Since
{\mathbf{S}}
is symmetric and
{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}
is a PSD matrix, we have

\begin{split}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}-{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}&={\mathbf{0}}\\
{\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}+{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}-{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}&={\mathbf{0}}.\end{split}

(26)

By summing two equations in (
26
), we get
{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}({\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}-{\mathbf{S}})+({\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}-{\mathbf{S}}){\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{0}}
, i.e.,
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}-{\mathbf{S}}
is a solution to the new Sylvester equation
{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}_{1}+{\mathbf{S}}_{1}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{0}}
with respect to
{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
. Now, we know again by
[
Bha13
, Theorem VII.2.1]
that
{\mathbf{0}}
is the unique solution to the system
{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}_{1}+{\mathbf{S}}_{1}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{0}}
. Thus,
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}={\mathbf{S}}
, and from (
26
), it further holds
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}
. This, together with (
24
) and the uniqueness of
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}
, asserts that
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}
is the unique solution to the linear system
\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}^{\top}
,
\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}+\widebar{{\mathbf{S}}}^{\top}={\mathbf{S}}
. This finishes the proof of this part.

Step 2.
Note that both
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
and
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
are of dimension
(pr-(r^{2}-r)/2)
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+^{\prime}}:\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}\longrightarrow\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
. Moreover, for any
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{S}}&{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{D}}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
, we have

\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+^{\prime}}(\xi_{\mathbf{X}}))=\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}})=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{P}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\top}{\mathbf{U}}+{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}&{\mathbf{P}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\\
{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}=\xi_{\mathbf{X}}.

(27)

Therefore,
\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}
is a bijection and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+^{\prime}}=(\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}})^{-1}
. At the same time, it is easy to check
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}
satisfies
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})={\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}
by observing
{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{P}}
.

Next, we provide the spectrum bounds for
\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}
. For any
\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}=({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{S}}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}){\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
, we have

\begin{split}\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})=\|\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}&\leq\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{1/2})(\|{\mathbf{S}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\rm F}^{2})\\
&=\sigma_{1}({\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1})(\|{\mathbf{S}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\rm F}^{2})\\
&=(\|{\mathbf{S}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\rm F}^{2})/\sigma_{r}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}),\end{split}

(28)

and

\begin{split}\langle{\mathbf{S}}^{\top},{\mathbf{S}}\rangle&\overset{(a)}{=}\langle{\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1},{\mathbf{S}}\rangle\\
&=\langle({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})^{1/2}{\mathbf{S}}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})^{-1/2},({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})^{1/2}{\mathbf{S}}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})^{-1/2}\rangle\geq 0,\end{split}

(29)

here (a) is because
{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}={\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}
by the construction of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
. Thus

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\|\xi^{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}_{{\mathbf{X}}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}&\overset{\eqref{def: xi-theta-correspondence-PSD1}}{=}\|{\mathbf{P}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}+{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&=\|{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{S}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\overset{\eqref{ineq: quadratic-S'-bound-PSD1}}{\geq}2(\|{\mathbf{S}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\rm F}^{2})\overset{\eqref{ineq: upper-bound-theta-norm}}{\geq}2\sigma_{r}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}),\end{split}

(30)

and

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\|\xi^{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}_{{\mathbf{X}}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}&\overset{\eqref{def: xi-theta-correspondence-PSD1}}{=}\|{\mathbf{P}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}+{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\leq 4\|{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&=2\|{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\leq 4\|\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=4\|\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{1/2}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\overset{\eqref{ineq: upper-bound-theta-norm}}{\leq}4\sigma_{1}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}).\end{split}

This finishes the proof of this proposition.
\blacksquare

Proposition 3

(Bijection Between
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
and
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p)
,
{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
and
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
. For any
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
and
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{S}}&{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{D}}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}\in T_{{\mathbf{X}}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
, define

\begin{split}\xi^{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}}_{{\mathbf{X}}}&:=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}\theta_{B}&{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\\
{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}\in T_{{\mathbf{X}}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+},\\
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}&:=[({\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1})^{\top}\quad{\mathbf{S}}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}.\end{split}

(31)

Then we can find a linear bijective mapping
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}
between
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
and
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}:\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}\longrightarrow\xi_{{\mathbf{X}}}^{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e},\\
&(\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+})^{-1}:\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}\to\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}\end{split}

such that
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
holds for any
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
.

Finally,
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}
satisfies the following spectrum bound:
\forall\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
,

\begin{split}(\sigma^{2}_{r}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1})\wedge 2\sigma_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})&\leq\|\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\quad\leq(\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}})\vee 2\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}).\end{split}

(32)

Proof of Proposition
3
.
First, it is easy to see
\xi^{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}}_{{\mathbf{X}}}
and
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}
are well defined given any
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
and
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\in T_{{\mathbf{X}}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
.

Next, we show
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}
is a bijection. Notice
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
is of dimension
(pr-(r^{2}-r)/2)
, which is the same with
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
. Suppose
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+^{\prime}}:\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}\longrightarrow\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
. For any
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{S}}&{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{D}}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
, we have

\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}(\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+^{\prime}}(\xi_{\mathbf{X}}))=\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}})=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}\theta_{B}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}&{\mathbf{B}}\theta^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\top}_{U}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\\
{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\theta_{U}^{\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}{\mathbf{B}}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}=\xi_{\mathbf{X}}.

(33)

Since
\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+^{\prime}}
are linear maps, (
33
) implies
\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}
is a bijection and
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+^{\prime}}=(\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}})^{-1}
. At the same time, it is easy to check
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
holds for any
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
.

Next, we provide the spectrum bounds for
\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}
. For any
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}=[({\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}})^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
, we have
\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})=\|{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B})=\|{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}
. Thus, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\overset{\eqref{def: xi-theta-correspondence-PSD2}}{=}\|\theta_{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\|_{\rm F}^{2}&=\|\theta_{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&=\|{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\theta_{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\leq(\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}})\vee 2\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\left(\|{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\\
&=(\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}})\vee 2\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\overset{\eqref{def: xi-theta-correspondence-PSD2}}{=}\|\theta_{\mathbf{B}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\|{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2}&\geq(\sigma^{2}_{r}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1})\wedge 2\sigma_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\left(\|{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\theta_{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{1/2}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\\
&=(\sigma^{2}_{r}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1})\wedge 2\sigma_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}).\quad\quad\quad\blacksquare\end{split}

Next, we present our first main result on the geometric landscape connections of Riemannian optimization (
1
) under the embedded and the quotient geometries.

Theorem 3

(Geometric Landscape Connections of (
1
) on
{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
and
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
)
.

Suppose the conditions in Proposition
2
hold and the
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}
in
\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}
satisfies
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. Then

\begin{split}{\rm grad}f({\mathbf{X}})&=\left(\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\dagger}+\left(\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\dagger}\right)^{\top}({\mathbf{I}}_{p}-{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\dagger})\right)/2,\\
\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}&=2{\rm grad}f({\mathbf{X}}){\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}.\end{split}

(34)

Furthermore, if
[{\mathbf{Y}}]
is a Riemannian FOSP of (
18a
), we have:

\begin{split}\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]&={\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}),\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})],\quad\forall\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}.\end{split}

(35)

Finally,
\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}
and
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})
have
(pr-(r^{2}-r)/2)
eigenvalues; for
i=1,\ldots,pr-(r^{2}-r)/2
,

\begin{split}&\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])})\text{ is sandwiched between }\\
&2\sigma_{r}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\lambda_{i}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))\text{ and }4\sigma_{1}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\lambda_{i}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})).\end{split}

Proof of Theorem
3
.
First, notice
{\mathbf{Y}}
lies in the column space spanned by
{\mathbf{U}}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\dagger}=P_{\mathbf{U}}
. So (
34
) is by direct calculation from the gradient expressions in Proposition
1
.

Next, we prove (
35
). Since
[{\mathbf{Y}}]
is a Riemannian FOSP of (
18a
), we have

\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}={\mathbf{0}},\quad\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}){\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{0}},

(36)

and have
{\rm grad}f({\mathbf{X}})={\mathbf{0}}
by (
34
). So
\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})=P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}
. Recall
{\mathbf{P}}={\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{Y}}
,
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}
and let
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}={\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{U}}
. Given any
\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathcal{H}_{{\mathbf{Y}}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
, we have

\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\rangle=\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\rangle,

(37)

where the equality is because
{\mathbf{P}}
is nonsingular,
{\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
and
\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})=P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}
.

Then by Proposition
1
:

\begin{split}&\quad\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]\\
&=\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top},{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}),\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\rangle\\
&\quad+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}){\mathbf{Y}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}],\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\rangle+\langle{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\left[\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}\right],\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\rangle/2\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: FOSP-condition-PSD1}}{=}\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{X}})[{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top},{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}),\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\rangle\\
&\overset{\text{Proposition }\ref {prop: psd-bijection1},\eqref{eq: Hessian-con-gradient-1}}{=}\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}),\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\rangle\\
&={\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}),\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})],\end{split}

where the last equality follows from the expression of
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})
in (
19
) and the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}
.

Then, by (
23
), (
35
) and Theorem
2
, we have
\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}
and
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})
have
(pr-(r^{2}-r)/2)
eigenvalues and
\widebar{\lambda}_{i}({\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}]))
is sandwiched between
2\sigma_{r}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\lambda_{i}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))
and
4\sigma_{1}({\mathbf{P}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\lambda_{i}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))
for
i=1,\ldots,pr-(r^{2}-r)/2
.
\blacksquare

Theorem 4

(Geometric Landscape Connections of (
1
) on
{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
and
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p)
,
{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
,
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}
,
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}
in
\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}
satisfies
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. Then

\begin{split}{\rm grad}f({\mathbf{X}})&=(\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{U}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})/2+(\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{U}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})^{\top}/2\\
&\quad+{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},\\
\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}&=\begin{bmatrix}2P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}{\rm grad}f({\mathbf{X}}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}}\\
{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\rm grad}f({\mathbf{X}}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}}\end{bmatrix}.\end{split}

(38)

Furthermore, if
[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}]
is a Riemannian FOSP of (
18b
), we have:

\begin{split}\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]&={\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}),\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})],\quad\forall\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}.\end{split}

(39)

Finally,
\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}
has
(pr-(r^{2}-r)/2)
eigenvalues and for
i=1,\ldots,pr-(r^{2}-r)/2
,

\begin{split}&\lambda_{i}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])})\text{ is sandwiched between }\\
&(\sigma^{2}_{r}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1})\wedge 2\sigma_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\lambda_{i}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))\text{ and }(\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}})\vee 2\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\lambda_{i}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})).\end{split}

Proof of Theorem
4
.
The proof of this theorem is similar to the proof of Theorem
3
and is postponed to Appendix
D
.
\blacksquare

and
