studied the algorithmic connection of Riemannian gradient descent type algorithms under the embedded geometry and a few quotient geometries in the PSD fixed-rank optimization problem. These works provide a good complement to the results in this paper.

There are many interesting extensions to the results in this paper to be explored in the future. First, as we have mentioned in the Example
1
in Section
3.1
, our results on the connection of Riemannian Hessians under the embedded and the quotient geometries are established at Riemannian FOSPs. It is interesting to explore whether it is possible to connect the landscapes under two geometries at non-stationary points. Second, other than the geometries covered in this paper, there are many other embedded and quotient geometries for fixed-rank matrices, such as the ones in
[
AIDLVH09
,
GP07
,
VAV13
]
for
{\cal M}_{r+}
, it will be interesting to study the geometric landscape connection of (
1
) and (
2
) under these geometries. Third, another common manifold that has both embedded and quotient representations is the Stiefel manifold
[
EAS98
]
. We believe our general procedure in Section
3
can also be used to establish the geometric connection in that setting as well. Finally, our ultimate goal is to better understand the connections and comparisons of different Riemannian geometries and give some guidelines on how to choose them given a Riemannian optimization problem. Some progress and discussions on how to choose Riemannian metrics in quotient geometries can be found in
[
MS16
,
VAV13
]
. While there is still not too much study on how to choose different quotient structures and manifold classes. It is important to explore these directions in the future.

Acknowledgments.

The authors would like to thank the editors and two reviewers for their great suggestions and comments. Y. Luo would like to thank Nicolás García Trillos for helpful discussions during the project.

Appendix A
Additional Preliminaries on Riemannian Connection

In this section, we provide a more detailed discussion of the Riemannian connection as it is critical in deriving the Riemannian Hessians. First, a
vector field

\xi
on a manifold
{\cal M}
is a mapping that assigns each point
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\cal M}
a tangent vector
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\in T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}
. The Riemannian gradient is a typical example of the vector field.

The
coordinate-free
definition of the Riemannian connection follows from the fundamental theorem of differential geometry
[
Pet06
, Chapter 2.1]
and can be characterized by the so-called
Koszul formula
given as follows:

2g_{\mathbf{X}}(\widebar{\nabla}_{\xi}\eta,\theta)={\rm D}g_{\mathbf{X}}(\eta,\theta)[\xi]+{\rm D}g_{\mathbf{X}}(\xi,\theta)[\eta]-{\rm D}g_{\mathbf{X}}(\eta,\xi)[\theta]+g_{\mathbf{X}}(\theta,[\xi,\eta])+g_{\mathbf{X}}(\eta,[\theta,\xi])-g_{\mathbf{X}}(\xi,[\eta,\theta]),

(71)

where
[\xi,\eta]
denotes the
Lie bracket
between vector fields
\xi
and
\eta
. If
{\cal M}
is an open subset of a vector space, the Lie bracket of vector fields is given by

[\xi,\eta]={\rm D}\eta[\xi]-{\rm D}\xi[\eta],

(72)

where
{\rm D}\eta[\xi]
is the classical direction derivative of a vector field in a vector space and its evaluation at
{\mathbf{X}}
is denoted by
({\rm D}\eta[\xi])_{\mathbf{X}}
and given as follows:
({\rm D}\eta[\xi])_{\mathbf{X}}=\lim_{t\to 0}(\eta_{{\mathbf{X}}+t\xi_{\mathbf{X}}}-\eta_{\mathbf{X}})/t

[
Mey11
, Section 3.5]
.

In the following Proposition
8
, we present the Koszul formulas associated with each simple manifold in Table
1
and their combinations. These formulas will be used in deriving Riemannian Hessians under the quotient geometries given in Sections
4.2.1
and
4.2.2
.

Proposition 8
.

(
Koszul Formulas Associated with
\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
,
{\rm St}(r,p)
,
\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
and their combinations
)

(i) (
Koszul Formulas Associated with
\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
,
{\rm St}(r,p)
and
\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
.
) Consider manifolds
\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
,
{\rm St}(r,p)
and
\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
endowed metrics
g
given in Table
1
with
{\mathbf{V}}_{\lozenge}={\mathbf{I}}
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{B}}^{-1}
. For vector fields
\xi,\eta,\theta
on
\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
or
{\rm St}(r,p)
or
\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
, we have the following Koszul formulas:

\begin{split}\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}:\,2g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\widebar{\nabla}_{\xi}\eta,\theta)&=2g_{\mathbf{Y}}({\rm D}\eta[\xi],\theta)+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi]\eta^{\top}\theta)+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\eta]\xi^{\top}\theta)-{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\theta]\eta^{\top}\xi),\\
{\rm St}(r,p):\,2g_{\mathbf{U}}(\widebar{\nabla}_{\xi}\eta,\theta)&=2g_{\mathbf{U}}({\rm D}\eta[\xi],\theta),\\
\mathbb{S}_{+}(r):\,2g_{\mathbf{B}}(\widebar{\nabla}_{\xi}\eta,\theta)&=2g_{\mathbf{B}}({\rm D}\eta[\xi],\theta)-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\xi{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\eta{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\theta)-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\eta{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\xi{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\theta).\end{split}

(73)

Here, the operation on vector fields is performed elementwise on the associated tangent vectors. For example,
\eta^{\top}\theta
is mapping that assign each point
{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
to the matrix
\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}
, i.e.,
(\eta^{\top}\theta)_{\mathbf{Y}}=\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}
.

(ii) (
Koszul Formula Associated with
{\rm St}(r,p)\times\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
.
)
Suppose we have metric
g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}
on
{\rm St}(r,p)\times\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
given by
g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{B}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B})
for
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}=[\eta_{U}^{\top}\quad\eta_{B}^{\top}]^{\top},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}]^{\top}\in T_{\mathbf{U}}{\rm St}(r,p)\times T_{\mathbf{B}}\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
. Then for vector fields
\xi,\eta,\theta
on
{\rm St}(r,p)\times\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
,

\begin{split}2g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\widebar{\nabla}_{\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})}}\eta,\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})})&=2g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}({\rm D}\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})}[\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})}],\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})})\\
&+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})+2{\rm tr}({\rm Sym}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]\eta_{B^{\prime}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}})\theta_{B^{\prime}})\\
&+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\eta_{B^{\prime}}]\xi_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})+2{\rm tr}({\rm Sym}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\eta_{B^{\prime}}]\xi_{B^{\prime}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}})\theta_{B^{\prime}})\\
&-{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\theta_{B^{\prime}}]\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\xi_{U^{\prime}})-2{\rm tr}({\rm Sym}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\theta_{B^{\prime}}]\eta_{B^{\prime}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}})\xi_{B^{\prime}}).\end{split}

(74)

(iii) (
Koszul Formula Associated with
{\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}_{*}
.
) Suppose we have metric
g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}
on
{\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}_{*}
given by
g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\eta_{Y}^{\top}\theta_{Y})
for
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[\eta_{U}^{\top}\quad\eta_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top}\in T_{\mathbf{U}}{\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times T_{\mathbf{Y}}\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}_{*}
. Then for vector fields
\xi,\eta,\theta
on
{\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}_{*}
,

\begin{split}2g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}(\widebar{\nabla}_{\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})}}\eta,\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})})&=2g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}({\rm D}\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})}[\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})}],\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})})\\
&+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi_{Y^{\prime}}]\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi_{Y^{\prime}}]\eta_{Y^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{Y^{\prime}})\\
&+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\eta_{Y^{\prime}}]\xi_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\eta_{Y^{\prime}}]\xi_{Y^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{Y^{\prime}})\\
&-{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\theta_{Y^{\prime}}]\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\xi_{U^{\prime}})-{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\theta_{Y^{\prime}}]\eta_{Y^{\prime}}^{\top}\xi_{Y^{\prime}}).\end{split}

(75)

Proof of Proposition
8
.

(Part (i))
The Koszul formulas associated with
{\rm St}(r,p)
and
\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
can be found in
[
Mey11
, Section 3.5.1]
and
[
Mey11
, Appendix B]
, respectively. Next, we derive the Koszul formula associated with the
\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
by using the general formula (
71
). First, for any vector fields
\eta,\theta,\xi
on
\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
, we have

\begin{split}({\rm D}g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,\theta)[\xi])_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}&=\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}+t\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}+t\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}}^{\top}\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}+t\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}})-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}})}{t}\\
&=\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}+t\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}+t\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}}^{\top}\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}+t\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}})-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}+t\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}}^{\top}\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}+t\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}})}{t}\\
&\quad+g_{\mathbf{Y}}({\rm D}\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}[\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}],\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}})+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}},{\rm D}\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}[\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}])\\
&={\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}]\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}})+g_{\mathbf{Y}}({\rm D}\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}[\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}],\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}})+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}},{\rm D}\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}[\xi_{{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime}}]).\end{split}

(76)

This proves

{\rm D}g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,\theta)[\xi]={\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi]\eta^{\top}\theta)+g_{\mathbf{Y}}({\rm D}\eta[\xi],\theta)+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,{\rm D}\theta[\xi]).

(77)

Then by (
71
) and the fact
\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}_{*}
is an open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}
, we have

\begin{split}&\quad 2g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\widebar{\nabla}_{\xi}\eta,\theta)\\
&={\rm D}g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,\theta)[\xi]+{\rm D}g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\xi,\theta)[\eta]-{\rm D}g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,\xi)[\theta]+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta,[\xi,\eta])+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,[\theta,\xi])-g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\xi,[\eta,\theta])\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: lie-bracket},\eqref{eq: directional-derivative-gY}}{=}{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi]\eta^{\top}\theta)+g_{\mathbf{Y}}({\rm D}\eta[\xi],\theta)+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,{\rm D}\theta[\xi])+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\eta]\xi^{\top}\theta)+g_{\mathbf{Y}}({\rm D}\xi[\eta],\theta)\\
&\quad+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\xi,{\rm D}\theta[\eta])-{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\theta]\eta^{\top}\xi)-g_{\mathbf{Y}}({\rm D}\eta[\theta],\xi)-g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,{\rm D}\xi[\theta])+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\theta,{\rm D}\eta[\xi]-{\rm D}\xi[\eta])\\
&\quad+g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta,{\rm D}\xi[\theta]-{\rm D}\theta[\xi])-g_{\mathbf{Y}}(\xi,{\rm D}\theta[\eta]-{\rm D}\eta[\theta])\\
&=2g_{\mathbf{Y}}({\rm D}\eta[\xi],\theta)+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi]\eta^{\top}\theta)+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\eta]\xi^{\top}\theta)-{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\theta]\eta^{\top}\xi).\end{split}

(78)

(Part (ii))
. For vector fields
\xi,\eta,\theta
on
{\rm St}(r,p)\times\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
,

\begin{split}&{\rm D}g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})})[\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})}]\\
=&\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{\begin{subarray}{c}{\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{B}}+t\xi_{B^{\prime}}}\eta_{U^{\prime}+t\xi_{U^{\prime}}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}+t\xi_{U^{\prime}}})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}+t\xi_{B^{\prime}}}\eta_{B^{\prime}+t\xi_{B^{\prime}}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}+t\xi_{B^{\prime}}}\theta_{B^{\prime}+t\xi_{B^{\prime}}})-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{B^{\prime}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B^{\prime}})\end{subarray}}{t}\\
=&{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]\eta_{B^{\prime}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{B^{\prime}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]\theta_{B^{\prime}})\\
&+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}\eta_{U^{\prime}}[\xi_{U^{\prime}}]^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{U^{\prime}}{\rm D}\theta_{U^{\prime}}[\xi_{U^{\prime}}])+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}\eta_{B^{\prime}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{B^{\prime}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}\theta_{B^{\prime}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}])\\
=&g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}({\rm D}\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})}[\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})}],\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})})+g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})},{\rm D}\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})}[\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime})}])\\
&+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]\eta_{B^{\prime}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{B^{\prime}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}[\xi_{B^{\prime}}]\theta_{B^{\prime}}).\end{split}

(79)

Combining (
71
) with (
79
), we conclude (
74
).

(Part (iii))
. For vector fields
\xi,\eta,\theta
on
{\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}_{*}
, we have

\begin{split}&{\rm D}g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})})[\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})}]\\
=&\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{{\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}+t\xi_{Y^{\prime}}}\eta_{U^{\prime}+t\xi_{U^{\prime}}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}+t\xi_{U^{\prime}}})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}+t\xi_{Y^{\prime}}}\eta_{Y^{\prime}+t\xi_{Y^{\prime}}}^{\top}\theta_{Y^{\prime}+t\xi_{Y^{\prime}}})-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\eta_{Y^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{Y^{\prime}})}{t}\\
=&{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi_{Y^{\prime}}]\eta_{U^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{U^{\prime}})+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}[\xi_{Y^{\prime}}]\eta_{Y^{\prime}}^{\top}\theta_{Y^{\prime}})\\
&+g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}({\rm D}\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})}[\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})}],\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})})+g_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})},{\rm D}\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})}[\xi_{({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{Y}}^{\prime})}]).\end{split}

(80)

Combining (
71
) with (
80
), we conclude (
75
). This finishes the proof.
\blacksquare
.

Appendix B
Additional Proofs in Section
3

B.1
Proof of Lemma
2
.

Since
\ell
is a diffeomorphism between
{\cal M}^{q}
and
{\cal M}^{e}
,
\ell
and
\ell^{-1}
are both open maps
[
Lee13
, Proposition 2.15]
. If
{\mathbf{X}}
is a minimizer of (
6
) in a neighborhood of
{\mathbf{X}}
say
{\cal N}_{1}
,
\ell^{-1}
maps
{\cal N}_{1}
to an open neighborhood of
\ell^{-1}({\mathbf{X}})
say
{\cal N}_{2}
, and
\ell^{-1}({\mathbf{X}})
is a minimizer of (
8
) for all
[{\mathbf{Z}}]\in{\cal N}_{2}
. This implies
\ell^{-1}({\mathbf{X}})
is a local minimizer of (
8
). This argument also applies in another direction.
\blacksquare

B.2
Proof of Theorem
2

First, because
\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])}
and
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})
are by definition self-adjoint linear maps from
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Z}}\widebar{{\cal M}}^{q}
and
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}^{e}
to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Z}}\widebar{{\cal M}}^{q}
and
T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}^{e}
, respectively, both
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})
and
\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])}
have
p
eigenvalues as
\dim(T_{\mathbf{X}}{\cal M}^{e})=\dim(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Z}}\widebar{{\cal M}}^{q})=d
. Suppose
{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{u}}_{d}
are eigenvectors corresponding to
\lambda_{1}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))
,
\ldots
,
\lambda_{p}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))
and
\mathbf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{d}
are eigenvectors corresponding to
\lambda_{1}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])}),\ldots,\lambda_{p}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])})
. For
k=1,\ldots,d
, define

\begin{split}&\mathcal{U}_{k}={\rm span}\{\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{k}\},\quad\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{k}={\rm span}\{\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{u}_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{u}_{k})\},\\
&\mathcal{V}_{k}={\rm span}\{\mathbf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{k}\},\quad\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{k}={\rm span}\{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{v}_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{v}_{k})\}.\end{split}

Let us first consider the case that
\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))\geq 0
. The Max-min theorem for eigenvalues (Lemma
13
) yields

\begin{split}\lambda_{k}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])})&\geq\min_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\neq 0}\frac{\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])}[\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}]}{\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\overset{\eqref{eq: general-setting-hessian-connection}}{=}\min_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\neq 0}\frac{{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}),\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})]}{\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\\
&=\min_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{U}_{k},\mathbf{u}\neq 0}\frac{{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}]}{\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{u}),\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{u}))}\geq\min_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{U}_{k},\mathbf{u}\neq{\mathbf{0}}}\frac{\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))g_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u})}{\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{u}),\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{u}))}\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: spectrum-bound-general-setting-L}}{\geq}\alpha\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))\geq 0.\end{split}

(81)

On the other hand, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: max-min-theorem}}{\geq}\min_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{0}}}\frac{{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime}),\mathcal{L}\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]}{g_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})}\overset{\eqref{eq: general-setting-hessian-connection}}{=}\min_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{0}}}\frac{\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])}[\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime}),\mathcal{L}^{-1}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime})]}{g_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbf{v}^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{\prime})}\\
&=\min_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{k},\mathbf{v}\neq{\mathbf{0}}}\frac{\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])}[\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}]}{g_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{v}),\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{v}))}\geq\min_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{V}_{k},\mathbf{v}\neq{\mathbf{0}}}\frac{\lambda_{k}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])})\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v})}{g_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{v}),\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{v}))}\\
&\overset{\eqref{ineq: spectrum-ineq1},\eqref{eq: spectrum-bound-general-setting-L}}{\geq}\lambda_{k}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])})/\beta.\end{split}

(82)

So we have proved the result for the case that
\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))\geq 0
. When
\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))<0
, we have
\lambda_{p+1-k}(-{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))=-\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))>0
. Following the same proof of (
81
) and (
82
), we have

\begin{split}-\lambda_{k}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])})=\lambda_{p+1-k}(-\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])})\geq\alpha\lambda_{p+1-k}(-{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))=-\alpha\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))>0,\\
-\lambda_{k}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))=\lambda_{p+1-k}(-{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))\geq\lambda_{p+1-k}(-\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])})/\beta=-\lambda_{k}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h([{\mathbf{Z}}])})/\beta.\end{split}

This finishes the proof of this theorem.
\blacksquare

Appendix C
Additional Proofs in Section
4

C.1
Proof of Lemma
4
.

It has been shown in
[
JBAS10
, Section 4]
that the vertical space of
\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
is
\{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}:\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{Y}}\boldsymbol{\Omega},\boldsymbol{\Omega}=-\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top}\}
with dimension
(r^{2}-r)/2
. By observing
{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{P}}
, it is easy to check

\begin{split}\{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}:\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\prime}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top},\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\prime}=-\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{{}^{\prime}\top}\}&=\{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}:\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\prime}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top},\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\prime}=-\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{{}^{\prime}\top}\}\\
&=\{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}:\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{Y}}\boldsymbol{\Omega},\boldsymbol{\Omega}=-\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top}\}=\mathcal{V}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}.\end{split}

In addition, notice
\dim(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}})=pr-(r^{2}-r)/2
and
(r^{2}-r)/2+(pr-(r^{2}-r)/2)=pr
, then
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
is the horizontal space if we can show it is orthogonal to
\mathcal{V}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
with respect to
\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}
. For
\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
and
\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}=({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{S}}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}){\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
,

\begin{split}\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})=\langle\boldsymbol{\Omega},{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\rangle\overset{(a)}{=}-\langle\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top},{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\rangle&\overset{(b)}{=}-\langle\boldsymbol{\Omega},{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}\rangle\\
&=-\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}),\end{split}

(83)

where (a) is because
\boldsymbol{\Omega}=-\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top}
and (b) is because
{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-\top}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}^{-1}
is symmetric by the construction of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
. (
83
) implies
\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})=0
. This finishes the proof for the first part.

From Section
2.1
, we know to show
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold endowed with the Riemannian metric
g^{r+}_{[{\mathbf{Y}}]}
induced from
\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}
, it is enough to show
\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}})=\bar{g}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}(\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}},\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}})
for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
, where
\eta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}
and
\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}},\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}
are the horizontal lifts of
\eta_{[{\mathbf{Y}}]}
and
\theta_{[{\mathbf{Y}}]}
at
{\mathbf{Y}}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}
, respectively. By Lemma
11
(i), we have the the horizontal lifts of
\eta_{[{\mathbf{Y}}]}
at
{\mathbf{Y}}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}
are related as
\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}=\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}}{\mathbf{O}}
. So

\bar{g}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}(\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}},\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}^{\top}\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}),

and
\bar{g}^{r+}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}(\eta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}},\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}})=\bar{g}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\eta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}})
holds for any
\eta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{{\mathbf{Y}}}
if and only if
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
holds for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. This shows
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold.
\blacksquare

C.2
Proof of Lemma
5

The expressions for vertical and horizontal spaces and their dimensions are provided in
[
BS10
, Theorem 1]
. Next, we prove statement (ii). From Section
2.1
, we know to show
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold endowed with the Riemannian metric
g^{r+}_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}]}
induced from
\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}
, it is enough to show
\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}})=\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})})
for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
, where
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}
and
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})}
are the horizontal lifts of
\eta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}]}
and
\theta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}]}
at
({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})
and
({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})
, respectively. By Lemma
11
(ii), we have the the horizontal lifts of
\theta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}]}
at
({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})
and
({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})
are related as
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})}=[(\theta_{U}{\mathbf{O}})^{\top}\quad({\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{O}})^{\top}]^{\top}
given
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}]^{\top}
. So

\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{O}})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\eta_{B}{\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{O}}),

and it is equal to
\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})
if and only if
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{B}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
hold for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. This finishes the proof.
\blacksquare

C.3
Proof of Lemma
6
.

The results under the full-rank factorization are provided in
[
MA20
, Proposition A.1]
. Next, we prove the results under the polar factorization. If
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}={\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
for some
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
, it is easy to check
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top}
.

Now, if
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top}
for some
{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p)
,
{\mathbf{B}}_{1},{\mathbf{B}}_{2}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
. Then the column spans of
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top}
are
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}
, respectively and they are the same. Hence there is
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
such that
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
. Moreover, since
{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p)
,
{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{O}}={\mathbf{I}}
, i.e.,
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. So
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
yield
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}={\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
.

Finally,
{\cal M}_{r+}=\{{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}:{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p),{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)\}
follows from the facts: (1)
{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\in{\cal M}_{r+}
for
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p),{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
and (2) any matrix
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\cal M}_{r+}
admits eigendecomposition which has form
{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}
.
\blacksquare

C.4
Proof of Lemma
7
.

The vertical space of
\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
was given in
[
AAM14
, Eq. (7)]
and it has dimension
r^{2}
. Moreover, we can check
\dim(\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}})=(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r
. If we can show
\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}\perp\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
with respect to
\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}
, then it implies
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
is a valid horizontal space choice. Suppose
\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{P}}_{2}^{-\top}\\
-{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{S}}^{\top}{\mathbf{P}}_{1}^{-\top}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
and
\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}=\begin{bmatrix}({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{P}}_{2}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}_{2}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}){\mathbf{P}}_{2}^{-\top}\\
({\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}{\mathbf{P}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}_{1}^{\top}+{\mathbf{V}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}){\mathbf{P}}_{1}^{-\top}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}}
, then by some simple calculations we have
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}^{r}(\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}{\mathbf{S}})-{\rm tr}({\mathbf{S}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}{\mathbf{S}})=0
.

To show
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{1}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold endowed with the Riemannian metric
g^{r}_{[{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}]}
, it is enough to show
\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})})=\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})})
for any
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\rm GL}(r)
, where
\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}
and
\eta_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})}
are the horizontal lifts of
\eta_{[{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}]}
and
\theta_{[{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}]}
at
({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})
and
({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})
, respectively. By Lemma
11
(iii), we have the the horizontal lifts of
\theta_{[{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}]}
at
({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})
and
({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})
are related as
\theta_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})}=[(\theta_{L}{\mathbf{M}})^{\top}\quad(\theta_{R}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})^{\top}]^{\top}
given
\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}=[\theta_{L}^{\top}\quad\theta_{R}^{\top}]^{\top}
. So

\begin{split}&\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})})\\
=&{\rm tr}\left({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}}(\eta_{L}{\mathbf{M}})^{\top}\theta_{L}{\mathbf{M}}\right)+{\rm tr}\left({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{M}},{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}}(\eta_{R}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top})^{\top}\theta_{R}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}\right),\end{split}

and it is equal to
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}^{r}(\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})})
for any
\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}
if and only if
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}}
satisfy the assumption given in the lemma. This shows
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{1}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold endowed with the Riemannian metric
g^{r}_{[{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}]}
. This finishes the proof.
\blacksquare

C.5
Proof of Lemma
8
.

The vertical space of
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{2}}
was given in
[
MMBS13
, Eq. (10)]
. Distinct from
[
MMBS13
]
, here the horizontal space in (
16
) is chosen in a non-canonical way, i.e., it is not orthogonal to
\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
with respect to
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}^{r}
, so we need to verify
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
in (
16
) is a valid horizontal space choice. To show this, we need to show
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}=\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
, where
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}=T_{\mathbf{U}}{\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times T_{\mathbf{B}}\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)\times T_{\mathbf{V}}{\rm St}(r,p_{2})
.

First, it is easy to check
\dim(\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}})=(r^{2}-r)/2
,
\dim(\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}})=(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r
and
\dim(T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}})=(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r+(r^{2}-r)/2
. So
\dim(\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}})+\dim(\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}})=\dim(T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}})
. Moreover, for
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\in\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
,
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
, we have
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}+\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\in T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
. Finally, given
\xi_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=[({\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}})^{\top}\quad{\mathbf{S}}\quad({\mathbf{V}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{V}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime})^{\top}]^{\top}
, we can find
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=[({\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1})^{\top}\quad({\mathbf{B}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1}{\mathbf{B}})^{\top}\quad({\mathbf{V}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1})^{\top}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
,
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=[({\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}+{\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{2})^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}\quad({\mathbf{V}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}-{\mathbf{V}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{2})^{\top}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}
such that
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}+\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=\xi_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1},\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{2},\theta_{B},{\mathbf{D}}_{1},{\mathbf{D}}_{2}
are uniquely determined by the equation system
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1}+\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{2}=\boldsymbol{\Omega},\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{2}=\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\prime},\theta_{B}={\mathbf{S}}-({\mathbf{B}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}),{\mathbf{D}}_{1}={\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}_{2}={\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}.
This proves
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}=\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{2}}

The claim
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{2}}
endowed with metric
g^{r}_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}}]}
induced from
\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold has appeared in
[
MMBS13
, Section 3]
without a proof, here we provide the proof for completeness. The result follows if we can show

\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})})=\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})})

holds for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
, where
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}
and
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})}
are the horizontal lifts of
\eta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}}]}
and
\theta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}}]}
at
({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})
and
({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})
, respectively. By Lemma
11
(iv), we have the the horizontal lifts of
\theta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}}]}
at
({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})
and
({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})
are related as
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})}=[(\theta_{U}{\mathbf{O}})^{\top}\quad({\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{O}})^{\top}\quad(\theta_{V}{\mathbf{O}})^{\top}]^{\top}
given
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}\quad\theta_{V}^{\top}]^{\top}
. So

\begin{split}&\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{O}})})\\
=&{\rm tr}({\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{O}})+{\rm tr}\left(({\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})^{-1}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\eta_{B}{\mathbf{O}}({\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{O}})^{-1}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{O}}\right)+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\eta_{V}^{\top}\theta_{V}{\mathbf{O}})\\
=&{\rm tr}(\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\eta_{B}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\theta_{B})+{\rm tr}(\eta_{V}^{\top}\theta_{V})=\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}^{r}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})}).\end{split}

This shows
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{2}}
endowed with the metric
g^{r}_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}}]}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold. This finishes the proof of this lemma.
\blacksquare

C.6
Proof of Lemma
9
.

The vertical space of
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{3}}
was given in
[
AAM14
, Eq. (33)]
. Distinct from
[
AAM14
]
, here the horizontal space in (
17
) is chosen in a non-canonical way, so we need to verify
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
in (
17
) is a valid horizontal space choice. To show this, we need to show
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}=\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
, where
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}=T_{\mathbf{U}}{\rm St}(r,p_{1})\times\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times r}_{*}
.

First, it is easy to check
\dim(\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}})=(r^{2}-r)/2
,
\dim(\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}})=(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r
and
\dim(T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}})=(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r+(r^{2}-r)/2
. So
\dim(\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}})+\dim(\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}})=\dim(T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}})
. Moreover, for
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\in\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
,
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
, we have
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}+\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\in T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
. Finally, given
\xi_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[({\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}+{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}})^{\top}\quad\xi_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top}
, we can find
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[({\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1})^{\top}\quad({\mathbf{Y}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1})^{\top}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
,
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[({\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{1})^{\top}\quad\theta_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
such that
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}+\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=\xi_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}
, where
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1},{\mathbf{D}}_{1},\theta_{Y}
are uniquely determined as by the equation system
\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1}=\boldsymbol{\Omega},{\mathbf{D}}_{1}={\mathbf{D}},\theta_{Y}=\xi_{Y}-{\mathbf{Y}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{1}.
This proves
T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}=\mathcal{V}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{3}}
.

To show
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{3}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold, we only need to show
\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})})=\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})})
for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
, where
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}
and
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})}
are the horizontal lifts of
\eta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}}]}
and
\theta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}}]}
at
({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})
and
({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})
, respectively. By Lemma
11
(v), we have the the horizontal lifts of
\eta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}}]}
at
({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})
and
({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})
are related as
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})}=[(\eta_{U}{\mathbf{O}})^{\top}\quad(\eta_{Y}{\mathbf{O}})^{\top}]^{\top}
given
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}=[\eta_{U}^{\top}\quad\eta_{Y}^{\top}]^{\top}
. So

\begin{split}\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{O}},{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}})})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{O}})+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\eta_{Y}^{\top}\theta_{Y}{\mathbf{O}}),\end{split}

and it is equal to
\bar{g}^{r}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})})
for any
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}
if and only if
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{O}}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}
holds for any
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. This shows
{\cal M}_{r}^{q_{3}}
is a Riemannian quotient manifold endowed with metric
g_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}}]}^{r}
induced from
\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})}^{r}
. This finishes the proof of this lemma.
\blacksquare

C.7
Proof of Lemma
10
.

(Full-rank factorization)
If
{\mathbf{L}}_{2}={\mathbf{L}}_{1}{\mathbf{M}}
and
{\mathbf{R}}_{2}={\mathbf{R}}_{1}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}
for some
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\rm GL}(r)
, it is easy to check
{\mathbf{L}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{L}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top}
.

Now, if
{\mathbf{L}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{L}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top}
for some
{\mathbf{L}}_{1},{\mathbf{L}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{1}\times r}
and
{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}
. Then the column spans of
{\mathbf{L}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{L}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top}
are
{\rm span}({\mathbf{L}}_{1})
and
{\rm span}({\mathbf{L}}_{2})
, respectively and they are the same. Hence there exists
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\rm GL}(r)
such that
{\mathbf{L}}_{2}={\mathbf{L}}_{1}{\mathbf{M}}
. Thus,
{\mathbf{L}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{L}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top}
yields
{\mathbf{R}}_{2}={\mathbf{R}}_{1}{\mathbf{M}}^{-\top}
.

Moreover,
{\cal M}_{r}=\{{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}:{\mathbf{L}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r}_{*},{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}\}
follows from the facts: (1)
{\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\in{\cal M}_{r}
for any
{\mathbf{L}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r}_{*},{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}
and (2) any matrix
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\cal M}_{r}
admits SVD
{\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}
and by taking
{\mathbf{L}}={\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}
and
{\mathbf{R}}={\mathbf{V}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{1/2}
, we have
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}
.

(Polar factorization)
If
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
,
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}={\mathbf{O}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{2}={\mathbf{V}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
for some
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
, it is easy to check
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}
.

Now, if
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}
for some
{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1})
,
{\mathbf{B}}_{1},{\mathbf{B}}_{2}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{2})
. Then the column spans of
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}
are
{\rm span}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})
and
{\rm span}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})
, respectively and they are the same. Hence there exists
{\mathbf{O}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
such that
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}_{1}
. Moreover, since
{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p)
,
{\mathbf{O}}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{O}}_{1}={\mathbf{I}}
, i.e.,
{\mathbf{O}}_{1}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. Similarly, we can conclude
{\mathbf{V}}_{2}={\mathbf{V}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}_{2}
for some
{\mathbf{O}}_{2}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. Thus,
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}
yields
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}={\mathbf{O}}_{1}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}_{2}
. Since
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r)
, by the characterization of PSD factorization in Lemma
6
, we know
{\mathbf{O}}_{1}={\mathbf{O}}_{2}
.

Moreover,
{\cal M}_{r}=\{{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}:{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1}),{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r),{\mathbf{V}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{2})\}
follows from the facts: (1)
{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}\in{\cal M}_{r}
for any
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1}),{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{S}_{+}(r),{\mathbf{V}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{2})
and (2) any matrix
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\cal M}_{r}
admits SVD which has form
{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}
.

(Subspace-projection factorization)
If
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}={\mathbf{Y}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
for some
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
, it is easy to check
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}^{\top}
.

Now, if
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}^{\top}
for some
{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1})
and
{\mathbf{Y}}_{1},{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}
. Then the column spans of
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}^{\top}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}^{\top}
are
{\rm span}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})
and
{\rm span}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})
, respectively and they are the same. Following the same argument as in the Polar factorization, we know
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
for some
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. Thus,
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{Y}}_{1}^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}^{\top}
yields
{\mathbf{Y}}_{2}={\mathbf{Y}}_{1}{\mathbf{O}}
.

Moreover,
{\cal M}_{r}=\{{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}:{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1}),{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}\}
follows from the facts: (1)
{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\in{\cal M}_{r}
for any
{\mathbf{U}}\in{\rm St}(r,p_{1}),{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}_{*}^{p_{2}\times r}
and (2) any matrix
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\cal M}_{r}
admits SVD
{\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}
and by taking
{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{V}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
, we have
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}
.
\blacksquare

Appendix D
Additional Proofs in Section
5

D.1
Proof of Proposition
1
.

The proof is divided into four steps: in Step 1, we derive the expressions for Riemannian gradients; in Step 2, we derive the Riemannian Hessian of (
1
) under the embedded geometry; in Step 3, we derive the Riemannian Hessian for
h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])
; in Step 4, we derive the Riemannian Hessian for
h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])
.

Step 1
. First, the Riemannian gradient expression of (
1
) under the embedded geometry can be found in
[
LLZ21
, Proposition 1]
. Next, we compute the Riemannian gradients under the quotient geometry. By definition

\begin{split}\langle{\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}}){\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}},\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}\rangle=\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}({\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}}),\eta_{\mathbf{Y}})&={\rm D}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})[\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}]=\langle\nabla\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}}),\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}\rangle\\
&=2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}){\mathbf{Y}},\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}\rangle,\quad\forall\eta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in T_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}},\end{split}

(84)

where
\nabla\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})
denotes the Euclidean gradient of
\bar{h}_{r+}
at
{\mathbf{Y}}
, and

\begin{split}&\langle{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{U}}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}){\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}},\eta_{U}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}){\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}},\eta_{B}\rangle\\
=&\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}({\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}),\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})\\
=&{\rm D}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})[\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]=\langle\nabla_{\mathbf{U}}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}),\eta_{U}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{\mathbf{B}}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}),\eta_{B}\rangle\\
=&2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}},\eta_{U}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}},\eta_{B}\rangle,\quad\forall\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}.\end{split}

(85)

From (
84
), we have the following expression for
{\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})
:

\begin{split}{\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})=\nabla\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}}){\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}=2\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}){\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}.\end{split}

(86)

Since
\mathcal{V}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}\perp\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
, by Lemma
1
, we have
\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}={\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})
.

At the same time, from (
85
), we have
{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})={\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}}
. In addition, suppose
{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{U}}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})={\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}+{\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}\in T_{\mathbf{U}}{\rm St}(r,p)
, then by (
85
), we have
P_{T_{\mathbf{U}}{\rm St}(r,p)}({\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}+{\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}-2\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}})=0
. Thus
{\mathbf{D}}=2{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}
and
\boldsymbol{\Omega}
is uniquely determined by the following Sylvester equation
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{\top}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}=2({\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}-{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}})
. So

\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}=P_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\mathcal{H}}({\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}))=\begin{bmatrix}2P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\\
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\end{bmatrix},

here
P_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\mathcal{H}}(\cdot)
is the projection operator onto
\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}^{q_{1}}_{r+}
and it satisfies
P_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\mathcal{H}}(\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})=[(P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\eta_{U})^{\top}\quad\eta_{B}^{\top}]^{\top}
for any
\eta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in T_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
.

Step 2.
Suppose
{\mathbf{X}}
has eigendecomposition
{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\prime}{\mathbf{U}}^{{}^{\prime}\top}
with
{\mathbf{U}}={\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{O}}
and
{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}
. Then for

\begin{split}\xi_{\mathbf{X}}&=[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{S}}&{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{D}}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top}=[{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{S}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}&{\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}&{\mathbf{0}}\end{bmatrix}[{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\quad{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}]^{\top},\end{split}

by
[
LLZ21
, Proposition 2]
we have in the PSD case

\begin{split}{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}},\xi_{\mathbf{X}}]&=\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}},\xi_{\mathbf{X}}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{{}^{\prime}-1}{\mathbf{O}}{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\rangle\\
&=\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}},\xi_{\mathbf{X}}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}{\mathbf{D}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{-1}{\mathbf{D}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\rangle\end{split}

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}={\mathbf{O}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{{}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{O}}={\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{U}}
.

Step 3.
We first compute

\begin{split}{\rm D}\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]={\rm D}{\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]&\overset{\eqref{eq: Rgradient-total-space-psd-quotient}}{=}\lim_{t\to 0}\left(\nabla\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}}+t\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}){\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{Y}}+t\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}^{-1}-\nabla\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}}){\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}\right)/t\\
&=\nabla\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}}){\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]+\nabla^{2}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}.\end{split}

(87)

By the definition of the bilinear form of the Riemannian Hessian, we have

\begin{split}\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]&=\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])[\theta_{[{\mathbf{Y}}]}]},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: quotient-hessian-linear-from}}{=}\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}\left(P_{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{\mathcal{H}}\left(\widebar{\nabla}_{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}\right),\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\right)\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: quotient-hessian-riemannian-connection-equal}}{=}\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}\left(\widebar{\nabla}_{\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}}\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\right)\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: koszul-formula-simple-manifolds}}{=}\bar{g}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}\left({\rm D}\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}],\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\right)+{\rm tr}\left({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\left[\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}\right]\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\right)/2\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: Rgradient-total-space-psd-quotient},\eqref{eq: directional-derivative-quotient-gradient-psd-1}}{=}\nabla^{2}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]+2{\rm tr}\left({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\left(\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}){\mathbf{Y}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]\right)^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\right)\\
&\quad+{\rm tr}\left({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{Y}}\left[\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}\right]\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\right)/2.\end{split}

(88)

Finally, we have
\nabla^{2}\bar{h}_{r+}({\mathbf{Y}})[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]=\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top},{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}+\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}),\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\rangle
from
[
LLZ21
, Proposition 2]
and plugging it into (
88
), we get the expression for the Riemannian Hessian of
h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])
.

Step 4.

For convenience, we denote
\bar{g}_{{\mathbf{U}}}^{r+}(\eta_{U},\theta_{U})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})
as the
{\mathbf{U}}
part inner product of
\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}
and
\bar{g}_{{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\eta_{B},\theta_{B})={\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{B}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B})
as the
{\mathbf{B}}
part inner product of
\bar{g}^{r+}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}
. We have

\begin{split}&\quad\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]\\
&=\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{r+}\left(\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])[\theta_{[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}]}]},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\right)\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: quotient-hessian-linear-from}}{=}\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{r+}\left(P_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{\mathcal{H}}\left(\widebar{\nabla}_{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}}\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}\right),\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\right)\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: quotient-hessian-riemannian-connection-equal}}{=}\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}^{r+}\left(\widebar{\nabla}_{\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}}\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\right)\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: koszul-formula-st-s+}}{=}\bar{g}_{{\mathbf{U}}}^{r+}\left({\rm D}\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{U}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}],\theta_{U}\right)+\bar{g}_{{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}\left({\rm D}\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}],\theta_{B}\right)\\
&\quad+{\rm tr}\left({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}]\theta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U}\right)/2\\
&\quad+{\rm tr}({\rm Sym}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}])\theta_{B}).\end{split}

(89)

Next, we compute
{\rm D}\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{U}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]
and
{\rm D}\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]
separately in (
89
).

\begin{split}&{\rm D}\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{U}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]\\
=&2\lim_{t\to 0}\Big(({\mathbf{I}}_{p}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U}})\nabla f\left(({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U})({\mathbf{B}}+t\theta_{B})({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U})^{\top}\right)({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U})({\mathbf{B}}+t\theta_{B}){\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}+t\theta_{B}}\\
&\quad\quad-P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}\Big)/t\\
=&-4{\rm Sym}({\mathbf{U}}\theta_{U}^{\top})\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}+2P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\left(\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}+\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}\right)\\
&+2P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]\\
&+2\lim_{t\to 0}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\left(\nabla f\left(({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U})({\mathbf{B}}+t\theta_{B})({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U})^{\top}\right)-\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})\right){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}/t\\
=&-4{\rm Sym}({\mathbf{U}}\theta_{U}^{\top})\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}+2P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\left(\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}+\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}\right)\\
&+2P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]\\
&+2P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}]{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}},\end{split}

(90)

and

\begin{split}&{\rm D}\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]\\
=&\lim_{t\to 0}\big({\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}+t\theta_{B}}({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U})^{\top}\nabla f\left(({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U})({\mathbf{B}}+t\theta_{B})({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U})^{\top}\right)({\mathbf{U}}+t\theta_{U}){\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}+t\theta_{B}}\\
&-{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}\big)/t\\
=&({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}\theta_{U}^{\top})\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}+{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})({\mathbf{U}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}})\\
&+{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}]{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}.\end{split}

(91)

By observing
f({\mathbf{X}})
is symmetric in
{\mathbf{X}}
,
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
and plugging (
90
) and (
91
) into (
89
), we have

\begin{split}&\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]\\
=&\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}),\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\rangle\\
&-4\langle{\rm Sym}({\mathbf{U}}\theta_{U}^{\top})\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}},\theta_{U}\rangle+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B},\theta_{U}\rangle\\
&+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}},\theta_{U}\rangle\\
&+\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}]\\
&+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}({\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}\theta_{U}^{\top})\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B})\\
&+{\rm tr}({\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})({\mathbf{U}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{B}}}){\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}\theta_{B})\\
&+{\rm tr}\left({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}]\theta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U}\right)/2\\
&+{\rm tr}({\rm Sym}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}])\theta_{B})\\
=&\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}),\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\rangle\\
&\,+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}},2\theta_{U}\theta_{B}+{\mathbf{U}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{B}}-\theta_{U}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}-{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\rangle\\
&\,+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}]\theta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})/2+{\rm tr}({\rm Sym}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}])\theta_{B}).\quad\blacksquare\end{split}

D.2
Proof of Theorem
4
.

First, (
38
) is by direct calculation from the gradient expressions in Proposition
1
. Next, we prove (
39
). Since
[{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}]
is a Riemannian FOSP of (
18b
), we have

\overline{{\rm grad}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}={\mathbf{0}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}}={\mathbf{0}}

(92)

and have
{\rm grad}f({\mathbf{X}})={\mathbf{0}}
by (
38
). So
\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})=P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}
. Given any
\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}=[\theta_{U}^{\top}\quad\theta_{B}^{\top}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{H}_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
, we have

\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\rangle=\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\rangle,

(93)

where the equality is because
\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})=P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\nabla f({\mathbf{X}})P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}
.

Then by Proposition
1
:

\begin{split}&\quad\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}[\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})},\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}]\\
&=\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}),\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\rangle\\
&\,+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathbf{U}},2\theta_{U}\theta_{B}+{\mathbf{U}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}{\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}[\theta_{B}]{\mathbf{B}}-\theta_{U}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}-{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\rangle\\
&\,+{\rm tr}({\rm D}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}]\theta_{U}^{\top}\theta_{U})/2+{\rm tr}({\rm Sym}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{B}{\rm D}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}[\overline{{\rm grad}_{\mathbf{B}}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}])\theta_{B})\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: FOSP-condition-PSD2}}{=}\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})[{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}+{\mathbf{U}}\theta_{B}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}]\\
&\quad+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}),\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}\rangle\\
&\overset{\text{Proposition }\ref {prop: psd-bijection2},\eqref{eq: Hessian-con-gradient-2}}{=}\nabla^{2}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}),\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})]+2\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{X}}),P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\theta_{U}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}\theta_{U}^{\top}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}}\rangle\\
&={\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})}),\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}(\theta_{({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}})})],\end{split}

where the last equality follows from the expression of
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})
in (
19
) and the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}}^{r+}
.

Then, by (
32
), (
39
) and Theorem
2
, we have
\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}])}
has
(pr-(r^{2}-r)/2)
eigenvalues and
\widebar{\lambda}_{i}({\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}}]))
is sandwiched between
(\sigma^{2}_{r}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1})\wedge 2\sigma_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\lambda_{i}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))
and
(\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{W}}^{-1}_{\mathbf{B}})\vee 2\sigma^{2}_{1}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}))\lambda_{i}({\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}}))
for
i=1,\ldots,pr-(r^{2}-r)/2
.
\blacksquare

D.3
Proof of Corollary
1
.

Here we prove the Riemannian FOSP, SOSP, and strict saddle equivalence on
{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
and
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
, similar proof applies to the equivalence of (
1
) on
{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
and
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{2}}
. First, by the connection of Riemannian gradients in (
34
), the connection of Riemannian FOSPs under two geometries clearly holds.

Suppose
[{\mathbf{Y}}]
is a Riemannian SOSP of (
18a
) and let
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}
. Given any
\xi_{\mathbf{X}}\in T_{{\mathbf{X}}}{\cal M}^{e}_{r+}
, we have
{\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\xi_{\mathbf{X}},\xi_{\mathbf{X}}]\overset{\eqref{eq: Hessian-connection-PSD1}}{=}\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}[(\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}})^{-1}(\xi_{\mathbf{X}}),(\mathcal{L}^{r+}_{\mathbf{Y}})^{-1}(\xi_{\mathbf{X}})]\geq 0
, where the inequality is by the SOSP assumption on
[{\mathbf{Y}}]
. Combining the fact
{\mathbf{X}}
is a Riemannian FOSP, this shows
{\mathbf{X}}={\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}
is a Riemannian SOSP under the embedded geometry.

Next, let us show the other direction: suppose
{\mathbf{X}}
is a Riemannian SOSP under the embedded geometry, there is a unique
[{\mathbf{Y}}]
such that
{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}={\mathbf{X}}
and it is a Riemannian SOSP of (
18a
). To see this, first the uniqueness of
[{\mathbf{Y}}]
is guaranteed by the fact
\bar{\ell}:{\mathbf{Y}}\in\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}\to{\mathbf{Y}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}\in{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
induces a diffeomorphism between
{\cal M}_{r+}^{e}
and
{\cal M}_{r+}^{q_{1}}

[
MA20
, Proposition A.7]
. In addition, we have shown
[{\mathbf{Y}}]
is a Riemannian FOSP of (
18a
). Then by (
35
), we have for any
\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{Y}}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r+}^{q_{1}}
,
\overline{{\rm Hess}\,h_{r+}([{\mathbf{Y}}])}[\theta_{\mathbf{Y}},\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}]={\rm Hess}f({\mathbf{X}})[\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}}),\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{r+}(\theta_{\mathbf{Y}})]\geq 0
.

Finally, the equivalence on strict saddles also follows easily from the sandwich inequality from Theorem
3
and the definition of the strict saddle.
\blacksquare

Appendix E
Additional Proofs in Section
6

E.1
Proof of Proposition
4
.

The proof is divided into five steps: in Step 1, we derive the expressions for Riemannian gradients; in Step 2, we derive the Riemannian Hessian of (
2
) under the embedded geometry; in Step 3, we derive the Riemannian Hessian for
h_{r}([{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}])
; in Step 4, we derive the Riemannian Hessian for
h_{r}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}}])
; in Step 5, we derive the Riemannian Hessian for
h_{r}([{\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}}])
.

Step 1.
First, the Riemannian gradient expression of (
2
) under the embedded geometry can be found in
[
LLZ21
, Proposition 1]
. Next, we compute
{\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})
,
{\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{V}})
and
{\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{U}},{\mathbf{Y}})
from their definitions:

\begin{split}&\quad\langle{\rm grad}_{{\mathbf{L}}}\,\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}){\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}},\eta_{L}\rangle+\langle{\rm grad}_{{\mathbf{R}}}\,\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}){\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}},\eta_{R}\rangle\\
&=\bar{g}_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}^{r}({\rm grad}\,\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}),\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})})\\
&={\rm D}\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})[\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}]=\langle\nabla_{{\mathbf{L}}}\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}),\eta_{L}\rangle+\langle\nabla_{{\mathbf{R}}}\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}}),\eta_{R}\rangle\\
&=\langle\nabla f({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}){\mathbf{R}},\eta_{L}\rangle+\langle(\nabla f({\mathbf{L}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}))^{\top}{\mathbf{L}},\eta_{R}\rangle,\quad\forall\eta_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\in T_{({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})}\widebar{{\cal M}}_{r}^{q_{1}},\end{split}

(94)

where
\nabla_{{\mathbf{L}}}\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})
denotes the Euclidean gradient of
\bar{h}_{r}({\mathbf{L}},{\mathbf{R}})
with respect to
{\mathbf{L}}
,
